Learning from Natural Images Untangles Number Sense

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Numerical cognition is a simple yet fundamental ability that remains challenging for modern vision-language models. Meanwhile, number sense is already present in numerically naïve animals, suggesting that it can emerge without explicit numerical learning. Deep neural networks provide a related observation, with numerosity-selective units appearing after training on unrelated natural-image tasks and even before any training. However, these findings leave unresolved whether visual learning contributes beyond the spontaneous emergence of numerical selectivity. Human development provides a clue, as children become better at making judgments in which numerosity and proportion conflict. Here, we introduce a geometric framework to trace how representations of these two quantities change across learning. Although untrained and pretrained networks exhibited similar unit-level numerosity selectivity and basic comparison performance, natural-image pretraining increased the angle between numerosity and proportion subspaces while reducing their overlap, supporting more robust readout under conflict. Direct manipulation of these geometric properties further demonstrated their functional contributions to decoding accuracy and functional independence. Importantly, conventional supervised pretraining was not essential for quantity separation, which also emerged through self-supervised learning without category labels and synthetic-image training without natural-image exposure. Together, our findings suggest that structured visual learning can refine spontaneously emerging number sense into robust and functionally independent quantity representations without explicit numerical training.

Benchmarking Open-Ended Vulnerability Discovery in Windows Kernel Binaries

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Frontier models are increasingly used to discover real-world security vulnerabilities, making it critical to systematically evaluate their cybersecurity capabilities. However, existing security benchmarks primarily focus on known vulnerabilities in open-source projects, largely overlooking closed-source binaries, which underpin much of the software industry. In this paper, we ask: how effective are frontier models at uncovering deep vulnerabilities in production binaries? To answer this question, we present DeepBinary, the first comprehensive benchmark for open-ended vulnerability discovery in Windows kernel binaries. Given a target binary, an agent analyzes it using a disassembler and kernel debugger in network-isolated VMs and reports each vulnerability with a crashing proof of concept (PoC). Unlike existing benchmarks, DeepBinary replays every report on fresh VMs and employs an LLM-based judge to assess its validity, uniqueness, and impact (estimated by bug bounty values). Beyond known vulnerabilities, DeepBinary also enables the discovery and validation of previously unknown (0-day) vulnerabilities. We conduct a comprehensive evaluation of representative open- and closed-weight models (including GPT-6 Sol, Claude Opus 5, and DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) across diverse coding agents (including Codex and Claude Code) on 12 target binaries. In total, they discover over 200 vulnerabilities with an estimated aggregate bounty of $661,000, including 146 distinct previously unknown vulnerabilities across all experiments. Our results further reveal substantial differences in models' ability to analyze binaries, conduct experiments, and sustain vulnerability discovery over long horizons. Overall, DeepBinary makes long-horizon, open-ended vulnerability discovery in complex binaries measurable, revealing fundamental differences in how frontier models analyze and explore such systems.

Neighbor Crowding Predicts Feature Stability in Sparse Autoencoders

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) offer an approach to identify language model representations by breaking apart polysemantic features into more sparse, interpretable ones. Yet, it is challenging to identify which of the learned features are actually stable, rather than artifacts of initialization. One approach is to train multiple SAE seeds, and compare the learned features across seeds by cosine similarity, but this is notably costly. This raises the question of whether stable features can be identified without the computational expense of training multiple seeds. In this work, we examine cross-seed feature stability across differing levels of training tokens, and demonstrate that in TopK SAEs, it is highly predictable using six computationally cheap statistics derived from a single training run. Neighbor crowding, a geometric property of the dictionary, is the strongest single predictor, with features that sit in dense regions of the unit sphere more likely to be stable than isolated ones.

Beyond the Efficiency-Efficacy Dilemma: A Critical Analysis of Transferability in Adversarial Malware Detection

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Machine Learning (ML) models are already being deployed at scale for malware detection. Therefore there is a pressing need to rigorously study adversarial robustness. To that end, we introduce the Adversarial AI Engine (AAIE): a platform for automated security stress-testing. We performed transfer attacks, via EMBER2018, against a Random Forest target. Counterintuitively, we found single-step FGSM to have the highest attack success rate (ASR) of 52.9% compared to iterative PGD with 45.2% (p < 0.002). Compared to iterative attacks which tend to overfit surrogate networks creating brittle perturbations, FGSM created more transferable examples. We benchmarked our attacks using ASR, transferability score, evasion rate, and resilience index. Through these metrics, proposed in concordance with emerging standards in AI governance, we expose shortcomings as our transferability scores peak at ~0.8 with constrained perturbations. We highlight the practical security implications of these findings and demonstrate AAIE for automated adversarial auditing.

ExtractBench Pro: The Long Tail of Document Extraction

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

We present ExtractBench Pro, a schema-guided document extraction benchmark that tests six challenging modes of extraction underrepresented in existing datasets. It is created following a framework for synthetic document generation, in which deterministic document generators assemble challenging components into documents that are designed to be difficult at a level calibrated for frontier systems. We synthesize 100 realistic documents spanning a diverse range of styles. On this data, we evaluate ten coding agents and eight extraction API configurations for accuracy, grounding, and their ability to perfectly extract documents. No extraction system extracts more than 61% of documents error-free. The strongest general-purpose coding agents, Claude Code Opus 5.5 and Codex GPT-6 Astra, outperform specialized extraction APIs on both perfect-document rate and Value F1, but at a higher cost per page. Among specialized extraction APIs, LlamaExtract spans the lower-cost frontier, with Agentic Plus Max Effort approaching the strongest coding agent on both perfect-document rate and Value F1 at lower cost per page. Our analysis of coding agent traces identifies intelligent image processing and programmatic reasoning, but also failures in applying document-specific definitions and recovering relationships from diagrams. We release ExtractBench Pro and these evaluation results as resources for the future development of improved frontier extraction systems.

ADClaw: A Programmable Visual Agent for Industrial Anomaly Detection and Understanding

📅 2026-11-25 15:51
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Industrial anomaly detection (IAD), the task of identifying and localizing deviations from normal product conditions, is essential to automated quality inspection in manufacturing. Many vision-language models for IAD rely on large domain-specific datasets for fine-tuning and make predictions in a single pass, leaving no opportunity to re-examine ambiguous regions. Although tool-using agents can revisit these regions, predefined tool interfaces limit how they can combine intermediate results in a follow-up check. When such checks are insufficient, models may miss subtle defects or misidentify the defect type. To address this limitation, we introduce ADClaw, an inspection agent that uses executable code to test anomaly hypotheses without task-specific fine-tuning. For each hypothesis, a Structured Normality Prior identifies the applicable rules and retrieves normal visual references for the inspection target. Guided by this prior, the agent writes and executes code in a persistent Inspection Workbench to combine masks, anomaly maps, and measurements from earlier steps into a new test of the suspected defect. After each check, Hypothesis-Guided Evidence Reasoning assesses whether the evidence supports the suspected defect and its location, using gaps or inconsistencies in that evidence to guide the next code action. ADClaw achieves competitive results in detection and localization across 2D and 3D industrial anomaly detection benchmarks and in anomaly understanding on MMAD.

ScanAR: Parallel Row-Scan Decoding for Efficient Image Generation in Unified Autoregressive Models

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Unified multimodal autoregressive (AR) models represent text and images in one token sequence and generate all modalities with a shared next-token prediction head. Their image generation, however, decodes an $H\times W$ visual grid token by token in raster order, which takes $HW$ sequential steps and dominates inference time. Existing acceleration methods for visual AR generation either change the tokenizer, the prediction head, or the model interface, or keep the model but cannot reach high parallelism without losing quality, so they do not carry over to unified AR models. We propose ScanAR, a row-scan parallel decoding method that only redefines the predecessors of visual tokens. The first visual row is generated from left to right as usual, and each later row is generated in a single parallel step, with every token predicted from the vertically aligned token of the previous row. Nonvisual tokens keep the standard autoregressive rule. ScanAR reduces the visual decoding depth from $HW$ to $H+W$ without changing the backbone, vocabulary, or output head and without adding parameters, and it supports both training from scratch and lightweight post-training. On Emu3.5-Image-34B, ScanAR post-training reaches a GenEval score of 82.36, the best among post-training acceleration methods trained on the same data, and generates $512\times512$ images $13.8\times$ faster than raster decoding under the same inference setting. On ImageNet, ScanAR trained from scratch has the highest throughput at every model size with FID comparable to raster LlamaGen. On the 34B Xiaomi-Robotics-U0 model, ScanAR preserves the overall quality of embodied scene generation, interleaved image-text generation, and video generation.

Collaborative Language Models for Semantic IoT Data Processing: Validation through Zero-Shot Human Activity Recognition

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Internet of Things (IoT) systems generate low-level sensor observations that are not directly suitable for process analysis and must first be transformed into semantically meaningful events. This paper investigates collaborative language models as a zero-shot semantic abstraction layer for converting such observations into higher-level event and activity labels. We propose a five-stage workflow comprising semantic mapping, semantic aggregation, temporal and contextual aggregation, specialized reasoning, and final event abstraction. The resulting labels and contextual information can subsequently be represented as event records for downstream Process Mining (PM) tasks. The approach is evaluated through Human Activity Recognition on the CASAS Aruba smart home dataset using eleven workflow configurations. The experiments show that collaboration alone does not guarantee improved performance: general-purpose auxiliary SLMs can degrade recognition, whereas assigning complementary specialist roles leads to better results and allows a smaller final classifier to retain much of the performance of a larger model. The selected workflow achieves a macro F1 of 0.643 in the 1.5-day comparison and 0.567 (weighted F1 0.680) in a one-week evaluation. Although trained ML approaches remain more accurate on this dataset, the results demonstrate a different design point: modular semantic event abstraction using off-the-shelf models without task-specific parameter fine-tuning. The findings indicate that the structure of LM collaboration and task decomposition is more important than simply increasing the number of participating models.

CADENCE: Sensor-GroundedWorld-Consistent Gaussian SLAM for Collaborative Mobile Reconstruction

📅 2026-11-25 15:26
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Collaborative reconstruction with consumer phones promises faster and more complete indoor maps. However, heterogeneous devices introduce degraded observations and unreliable cross-device associations, so a single poor contributor or incorrect match can corrupt the shared Gaussian map. To address this, we propose \textbf{CADENCE}, a collaborative Gaussian simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM) system that separates finding cross-device matches from deciding whether and how peer observations update the map. To find matches, a sensor-grounded regional memory summarizes committed observations and ranks candidate peer regions using appearance, relative pose, gravity, barometric altitude, and observation quality. Deterministic geometric verification and optional physical checks then decide which of the proposed matches are accepted. Once a match is accepted, separate cue-based confidences control how its observations contribute to pose constraints, geometry, appearance, and memory. As a result, useful depth can still contribute even when color is unreliable. Throughout this process, local tracking remains isolated from mapping, verification, and networking. We evaluate CADENCE on eight Matterport3D houses that were not used to train the memory. On these houses, regional memory achieves an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of $0.95$ to $0.97$ and thus outperforms appearance retrieval, while verification keeps false-link rates below $0.5\,\%$. Furthermore, soft confidence weighting reduces the mean receiver peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) loss from $2.66$ to $1.05$\,dB across eight degradations while retaining useful peer depth. Finally, using inertial and platform motion, CADENCE tracks all twelve held-out iPhone scans with a mean absolute trajectory error of $6.98$\,cm, and its refined maps achieve better average rendering quality than the baselines.

MolGPAO: Molecular Generative Pre-trained Autoregressive Octree with Speculative Decoding and Sub-Millisecond Seam Healing for 3D Molecules

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Autoregressive generation over adaptive 3D octrees (Uni-3DAR) offers an intrinsic $O(N \log N)$ sparsity advantage over dense $O(N^2)$ equivariant diffusion and flow models on large drug-like molecules, yet lags in geometric equilibrium, closed-shell valence stability, and property fidelity due to Morton-seam coordinate jumps, leaf-heavy compute with root-count trapping, and target-shortcut collapse in internal critics. We introduce MolGPAO (Molecular Generative Pre-trained Autoregressive Octree), a standalone 3D octree architecture and single-output speculative tree-decoding engine using zero external surrogates, zero reinforcement learning, and zero inference-time MLIP relaxation. MolGPAO unifies: (1) non-trapping 6-D ScaleFreeBudgetEmbedding with L5-to-L1 covalent packing priors; (2) a FlashAttention-2 pause-and-gather speculative octree funnel ($m_{s1} \to m_{s2} \to 1$, $M_{\mathrm{out}} = 1$); (3) an anti-shortcut ground-truth TwoStageOctreeProcessCritic with 64-RBF covalent edge gating and $SO(3)$-invariant 12-channel multipole norms (blind QM9 $r = 0.9867$--$0.9989$); (4) a $<0.5$ ms 4-layer $SE(3)$ PostTreeGraphRefiner for Morton-seam healing, expected-XYZ bonded supervision, selective CFG, and zero-heavy-displacement valence reconciliation; and (5) bubble-free stride-2 GPU leaf decoding. Across GEOM-Drugs (0.49 GPU-h), disjoint 50k/50k QM9 (0.43 GPU-h), and PBE0/def2-TZVP-verified OE62 (0.56 GPU-h), MolGPAO lifts drug-scale valid-connected yield 6.78x (12.7% to 86.2%, 99.83% atom stability), runs 7.3x faster (or 1.65x faster at 2.0x yield) than 100-step FlowMol at $N=100$, beats 156 GPU-h PropMolFlow on QM9 HOMO (-12.7%) and dipole (-21.0%) at 362x lower compute, and doubles live DFT hit rate on OE62 (33.3% vs. 16.7%) at 92.8x lower compute.

The cost of being seen as AI in group bargaining

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

As artificial intelligence (AI) becomes embedded in collective decision-making, people's perceptions of AI may shape who reaches agreement and what they receive. We studied this in a repeated three-player majoritarian bargaining game with 2,234 participants. Players saw each opponent identified as Human or AI. The instructions described AI as acting either independently or on behalf of a human. Human participants made every decision. In mixed groups, players presented as AI received fewer points in opening offers, were included in fewer final agreements and earned about 18% less per round than those presented as Human. Smaller payoffs in agreements that included them accounted for most of the observed gap. Further analysis found a payoff gap even when all three players agreed. Social perceptions of AI may therefore shape who benefits from collective decisions, with potential costs for people represented by AI.

Evacuation Traffic Prediction for Dynamic Transportation Networks over Multiple Hurricanes: A Generalized Graph Learning Approach

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

High traffic demand during hurricane evacuations causes severe congestion on major highways, leading to delays and safety risks for evacuees. Predicting evacuation traffic in real-time can significantly enhance evacuation management efforts. However, evacuation traffic is highly dynamic and uncertain due to evolving hurricane characteristics and diverse population response. Each hurricane has unique characteristics and evacuation contexts, resulting in different spatial-temporal traffic patterns. Moreover, the structure of detector-based transportation networks varies across multiple hurricanes. Hence, a model trained on the data from one hurricane may not generalize well for others. This study proposes a generalized dynamic graph learning framework using traffic detectors and evacuation data from 12 historical hurricanes. Leveraging the dynamic nature of transportation network, we develop a Graph Convolutional Long Short-Term Memory (GCN-LSTM) model that predicts evacuation traffic up to 6 hours in advance. For an unseen hurricane (Milton, 2024), the model shows strong generalizability, achieving an RMSE of 280.6 and R2 of 0.96 for 1-hour prediction, and an RMSE of 459.8 and R2 of 0.89 for overall 6-hours prediction. We also introduce a modular training approach, where we evaluate performance improvements by sequentially incorporating data from multiple hurricanes. The proposed model outperforms state-of-the-art baseline models for overall 6-hour prediction horizons. Ablation experiments show an 8.4% improvement in RMSE for the proposed dynamic GCN-LSTM model compared to its static counterpart. Traffic management officials can use the developed model to understand future traffic conditions during hurricane evacuation in advance and take proactive measures to reduce delays during evacuation.

Onnx2TEE: Automated Graph-Aware Model Partitioning for TEE-based Edge AI Protection

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

AI models are increasingly being deployed at the edge. Since these models often require significant research and development to produce, are often deployed in potentially hostile environments, and downstream systems often rely on accurate data; protecting them from theft or manipulation is worthy of serious consideration. Trusted Execution Environments (TEEs), which provide hardware-enforced separation of software execution, can protect model weights but impose strict resource constraints, making full-model execution impractical for large neural networks. We present Onnx2TEE, an automated, model-independent pipeline that partitions ONNX models across rich, trusted execution environments for non-sequential feed-forward networks using graph-aware segmentation to preserve computational dependencies while minimizing boundary communication and reducing TEE-induced inference latency. Evaluation on an NVIDIA Jetson AGX Orin using an Arm TrustZone TEE demonstrates that model parameters can be highly concentrated in individual segments, supporting selective protection of parameter-dense model components. The evaluation further shows that the generated trusted app's memory requirements depend on both computation and intermediate tensor sizes.

CARROT: Competitive Adaptive Graph Clustering via Semi-Relaxed Optimal Transport

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Deep graph clustering (DGC) has emerged as a prominent paradigm for unsupervised attributed graph clustering. However, existing methods often struggle with two practical challenges encountered in real-world applications: the number of clusters $K$ is unknown a priori, and cluster sizes are frequently highly imbalanced. Methods relying on a pre-specified $K$ may fail to recover the underlying clustering structure when $K$ is misspecified, while many approaches implicitly favor balanced partitions, hindering the discovery of minority clusters. We propose CARROT, a Competitive Adaptive gRaph clustering framework via semi-Relaxed Optimal Transport, which introduces semi-relaxed optimal transport into deep graph clustering to jointly address both challenges. CARROT first pre-trains node embeddings to establish a meaningful embedding space, and then jointly optimizes embeddings and cluster assignments through an Adaptive Clustering Mechanism. The mechanism combines semi-relaxed optimal transport to adapt cluster capacities to the underlying data, a competition loss to concentrate mass onto well-supported cluster prototypes, thereby starving degenerate ones, and hard pruning to progressively eliminate them. Extensive experiments on five public benchmarks demonstrate that CARROT achieves state-of-the-art NMI and ARI, accurately recovers the number of clusters without supervision, and faithfully preserves size-imbalanced community structures.

SemProxy: Semantic 3D Proxies for Controllable Agentic 3D Modeling

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Coding agents are increasingly capable of generating 3D models, yet enabling users to effectively control the modeling process remains challenging. Existing workflows rely primarily on text prompts and reference images, which often struggle to convey precise spatial intentions and require iterative corrections. We introduce SemProxy, a lightweight intermediate representation that bridges intuitive human interaction and agentic 3D modeling. Instantiated as labeled 3D bounding boxes, semantic 3D proxies allow users to directly specify object parts and spatial arrangements while leaving geometric details to coding agents. These proxies can be serialized into structured text and rendered as images, making them directly accessible to existing coding agents without additional training or modifications. We further develop ProxyStudio, a user interface that integrates text, image references, and interactive 3D proxy editing into agentic modeling workflows. We demonstrate the versatility of SemProxy across object- and scene-level modeling tasks with different coding agents, and evaluate its effectiveness in supporting controllable human-agent collaborative 3D modeling through a user study.

SceneDesigner++: A Unified 9-DoF Pose Control Framework for T2I and T2V Generation

📅 2026-11-25 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Precise 9-DoF pose control is essential for visual content generation, enabling users to specify object location, size, and orientation in 3D space. However, existing methods typically focus on either text-to-image (T2I) or text-to-video (T2V) generation, with limitations in pose controllability and temporal coherence. We present SceneDesigner++, a unified framework for 9-DoF pose control across both T2I and T2V generation. We construct ObjectPose9D++, a dataset containing high-quality images and videos with accurate pose annotations. Trained on ObjectPose9D++, SceneDesigner++ combines absolute and relative pose encoding strategies to decouple frame-wise spatial grounding from inter-frame motion modeling. Specifically, absolute pose encoding leverages CNOCS map sequences to provide explicit, frame-wise 9-DoF pose guidance. Meanwhile, our relative pose encoding, termed VPRoPE, incorporates relative pose information into rotary position embeddings to explicitly model inter-frame pose transitions, thereby promoting temporally coherent object motion. A learnable orthogonal transformation is further introduced for each attention head, allowing different heads to capture complementary geometric relationships while preserving input feature norms to promote training stability. We further adopt a two-stage training strategy to learn the absolute and relative pose encoding mechanisms separately. Benefiting from this lightweight and decoupled design, SceneDesigner++ also supports integration with an independently trained camera-control module, enabling separate or joint control over object and camera motion. Finally, we introduce T2IVPoseEval, a benchmark for evaluating diverse object poses and motion patterns in both T2I and T2V tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SceneDesigner++ consistently outperforms same-backbone baselines in pose fidelity and motion quality.

Aligned Machine: A Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Embedding Similarity via Human Comparative Judgements

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Embedding models increasingly inform how people search, recommend, and retrieve information, yet we lack credible ways to test whether these similarity judgments reflect how humans understand meaning. Existing benchmarks typically rely on static, expert-labeled pairs with coarse Likert-style annotations, limiting scalability and making it difficult to obtain consistent, high-confidence annotations. We introduce Aligned Machine, an interactive, public-facing platform that transforms broad participation into a continuously growing Human-Aligned Similarity Benchmark (HASB) for evaluating semantic alignment in embedding-based systems. Participants complete a cognitively-natural, forced-choice task, comparing two pairs of items (text or images) and selecting which pair is more similar. This comparative design is accessible to non-experts, yields clean preference data, and supports robust aggregation. We report results from an initial deployment with 115 participants contributing 2,875 total labeled pairs and 872 unique pairs. Using HASB, we evaluated 12 widely used embedding models and found only partial alignment between embedding similarity and human judgments. Text-only models consistently achieve higher overall agreement than multimodal models, while multimodal performance varies systematically by task type: alignment is strongest for image-image comparisons, weaker for image-text comparisons, and lowest for text-text comparisons. Aligned Machine is designed to evolve over time as participation grows, supporting longitudinal analysis, domain-specific studies, bias detection, and measurement of concept drift across model versions. By combining accessible public participation with rigorous comparative evaluation, HASB provides a scalable and accountable framework for auditing semantic alignment in embedding models.

Physics-Guided Generative Modelling of Oxy-Combustion Flame Images: From Conditional LSGANs to Denoising Diffusion Models

📅 2026-11-25 15:35
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Validating industrial oxy-combustion burner designs traditionally requires costly experimental campaigns in which each operating condition is physically reproduced on an instrumented rig and imaged via OH* chemiluminescence, rendering iterative design exploration prohibitively slow. This work develops deep generative surrogate models that map a 15-dimensional vector of continuous operating parameters (thermal power, swirl numbers, momentum ratios, mixture fractions, etc.) directly to a $512\times64$ heat-release flame image, trained on 1,609 experimental samples from the OXYTEC dual-swirl non-premixed oxy-combustion database. Two complementary generative paradigms are investigated, as they offer opposite strengths for surrogate modelling: a custom spatially-anchored conditional least-squares GAN (cLSGAN-L1-CBN), whose single-pass generation enables near-instantaneous inference, and a custom physics-guided denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM), whose iterative sampling favours the smooth, continuous heat-release gradients of fluid flows. Both are validated through ablation on a stratified held-out test set. The optimized cLSGAN reaches RMSE 0.022 and FID 20.68 and generates an image in about 20 ms on a mobile workstation GPU, though extended training leaves its texture realism unchanged; the DDPM improves steadily over long training horizons, reaching RMSE 0.019 and FID 20.29 at 40,000 epochs, at the cost of roughly thirty seconds per image on the same hardware --- three orders of magnitude slower than the adversarial surrogate. A nearest-neighbour analysis traces the persistent worst-case errors to sparsely sampled regions of the parameter space, and a parameter-sensitivity study confirms smooth, physically consistent responses to perturbed inputs. These results demonstrate that carefully guided generative models can act as reliable visual surrogates for combustion experiments.

Multi-Party Authorization of Maintenance Prescriptions on a Permissioned Blockchain

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

In industrial prescriptive maintenance, an equipment supplier's model may recommend an intervention that a plant schedules, a contractor executes, and a quality or safety function approves. A shared ledger records who submitted the prescription, yet cannot check whether the agreed model and decision rule produced it. We require permissioned-blockchain endorsers to verify a succinct proof of that calculation before releasing a work order. On a four-organization Hyperledger Fabric testbed using a public engine benchmark, this stopped all nine contested or mis-operated prescriptions, whereas hash checks accepted four that either raised simulated maintenance cost or reintroduced late failures.

Towards Closing the Performance Gap in Cross-Modal Place Recognition

📅 2026-11-25 18:38
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Cross-modal place recognition (CMPR) matches natural language descriptions against databases of geo-tagged reference images, enabling localization without visual queries. Despite its potential for language-guided navigation, existing vision-language models lag far behind visual place recognition (VPR) baselines, even after task adaptation. Unlike general-purpose text-to-image retrieval, place recognition relies on subtle local cues to distinguish visually similar locations. We therefore hypothesize that a primary bottleneck in CMPR is capturing fine-grained correspondences between image regions and text details, which requires higher visual token granularity, strong text representation capacity, and local cross-modal matching. To address this challenge, we propose CrossPlace, a two-stage CMPR framework. Stage 1 performs global retrieval by pairing a jointly aligned DINOv2-B/14 visual backbone with a progressively fine-tuned BGE-Large text encoder, while Stage 2 refines top candidates using a lightweight token-level cross-attention reranker. Through systematic analysis, we isolate the drivers of retrieval accuracy and show that visual token density provides the largest performance improvement, while text encoder capacity and task fine-tuning offer complementary gains. To evaluate robustness under realistic conditions, we also extend CMPR evaluation to city-scale and severe weather and illumination settings. Across all existing and new benchmarks, CrossPlace achieves double-digit Recall@1 improvements over prior cross-modal methods, closes the gap to unimodal text retrieval, and narrows it to unimodal VPR. Code and benchmarks are available at https://github.com/oferidan1/CrossPlace

ProManO: Beyond Sim(3) for Metric Upgrade of Feed-Forward Geometry with Visual-Inertial Anchors

📅 2026-11-25 14:43
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Upgrading feed-forward geometry to metric scale with visual-inertial odometry (VIO) raises the question of which correction should align dense feed-forward prediction with sparse metric VIO anchors. Existing methods use either a similarity transformation, which cannot represent depth-dependent distortion, or a projective transformation, which alters pre-calibrated intrinsics. We prove that the transformations compatible with pre-calibrated intrinsics form a ten-dimensional manifold. Beyond Sim(3), this manifold contains three degrees of freedom (DoF) that locate the plane at infinity and leave residuals growing quadratically with depth range after any similarity alignment. These DoF are invisible in the image and can be resolved only by metric depth along viewing rays or by multi-view constraints. To estimate them, we propose ProManO, a probabilistic manifold optimization framework in which every anchor is weighted by its along-ray uncertainty and inlier probability within a single expectation-maximization (EM) procedure. ProManO certifies the similarity synchronization and refines the plane at infinity only where it is observable, which guarantees an objective value no worse than that of the optimal similarity alignment. Experiments on EuRoC, CID-SIMS, Oxford Spires, and KITTI-360 demonstrate that ProManO achieves the highest mean F1 and outperforms Sim(3) alignment on all four datasets. The project page is available at https://h0xg.github.io/ProManO/.

UniMoE-CP: Unified Conditional Waveform Prediction Across Measured Optical Wireless Links

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Shared prediction across measured optical wireless links requires a common model to accommodate link-dependent waveform dynamics. This paper studies unified learning for underwater optical, 100-m visible-light communication and 4.3-km free-space optical links. We formulate received-waveform forecasting from historical observations and known future transmitted excitation using one shared parameter set. UniMoE-CP combines a shared pool of temporal experts, window-dependent routing and spectral covariate fusion with an autoregressive decoder. The design permits conditional specialization while reusing expert parameters across the measured links. Pooled-training benchmarks evaluate each link separately and report an aggregated result using normalized mean square error (NMSE) and normalized mean absolute error (NMAE). The reported average NMSE reductions are 29.9% for 100-m VLC, 29.1% underwater and 21.4% for 4.3-km FSO. The aggregated average NMSE decreases from 0.1645 for the best baseline to 0.1178 for UniMoE-CP.

Enhancing Maritime Domain Awareness using Latent Space Representations

📅 2026-11-25 18:51
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

This work presents a novel application of Earth Observation (EO) embeddings for Maritime Domain Awareness (MDA) from the perspective of the geospatial foundation model TerraMind. By operating directly on compressed latent-space representations rather than on the original Sentinel-1 imagery, this approach directly addresses the data gravity bottleneck that increasingly constrains operational EO processing, in line with the objectives of the Embed2Scale project. In particular, linear and non-linear probing of compressed embeddings, latent space representations of synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery, is used to detect, localise, and characterise vessels from Sentinel-1 GRD images. Vessel detection achieves a ROC-AUC of 0.95, while vessel localisation has a median error of around 300 m for vessels longer than 100 m, and vessel AIS type-code classification achieves 62% accuracy for the majority classes. The latter, however, is highly correlated with vessel length. These results reveal both the potential and current limitations of Geospatial Foundation Models (GeoFMs) when applied to fine-grained MDA understanding in real-world scenarios.
Additionally, a new maritime remote sensing dataset is released, pairing about 2,000 Sentinel-1 images with temporally aligned AIS signals from commercial vessels. Through manual inspection, each candidate sample was verified for bounding-box quality and for consistency between the AIS-reported vessel length and the observed vessel extent, cross-checking vessel identity and location against publicly available vessel records and, where available, photographs. The dataset was made ready for use with the NeuCo-Bench neural compression benchmark framework, publicly available on HuggingFace through https://huggingface.co/datasets/embed2scale/S1-single-ship-downstream .

DuCo: Exploiting Dual Cross-Layer Consistency for Sparse Geometric Attention

📅 2026-11-25 15:59
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Dense global attention is crucial for capturing cross-view geometry in visual geometry foundation models, yet its quadratic complexity limits efficient inference on long image sequences. Existing sparse attention methods primarily select entries of the global attention matrix based on attention probabilities and construct masks independently across layers, overlooking value redundancy and cross-layer information reuse. By analyzing attention distributions and clustering value representations across layers, we reveal two forms of \emph{cross-layer consistency}, characterized by highly similar attention probability distributions and localized changes in value redundancy structures. Building on these observations, we propose \textbf{DuCo}, a training-free sparse attention framework that exploits historical attention information and value redundancy for efficient geometric inference. Specifically, DuCo leverages historical attention information to estimate current-layer dense attention probabilities and introduces value clustering to exploit redundancy, with clusters efficiently refined through local updates across layers. Both factors are integrated through probability-weighted value distortion to jointly optimize four cluster representation states under an attention budget for sparse mask construction. Experiments across three visual geometry foundation models and five benchmarks demonstrate favorable accuracy--efficiency trade-offs.

Beyond Feature Fusion: Sensor-Grounded Semantic-Spiking Learning for Robust Object Detection

📅 2026-11-25 18:10
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Underexposure weakens object details, and overexposure obscures boundaries. Spike cameras encode light into high-frequency binary streams. Long-window spike counts give stable appearance cues, while short observations reveal changes in firing rates from sparse measurements. We propose SPIRE, a sensor-grounded semantic-spiking framework for illumination-robust object detection from a single spike stream. SPIRE builds an appearance view from the full packet through detection-oriented illumination adaptation and constructs a sequence of cumulative views from progressively longer prefixes. Each prefix supplies a rate map, an edge map, and a rate difference that tracks changes as spikes accumulate. At selected scales, ANN features and spike-derived initialization jointly set the initial membrane potentials and firing thresholds, allowing semantic cues to shape the responses during temporal encoding. Local spike-count contrasts supervise both branches during training. The residual between measured and ANN-predicted contrasts combines with the spiking features to produce a bounded correction to the fused feature pyramid. On simulated VOC and COCO, SPIRE-D outperforms the strongest baseline by 4.00-5.50 AP50 points across the four under- and overexposure settings. On real PKU-Vidar-DVS recordings, it achieves 67.07 AP50 and 40.10 AP75. SPIRE-D also uses 50.4% fewer MACs than SPFD.

KeyDeck: Multi-Paper-to-Slide Generation via Cross-Paper Synthesis and Reference-Guided Personalization

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Multi-paper-to-slide generation (multi-PTSG) is the task of constructing a unified presentation from multiple research papers by selecting, organizing, and synthesizing information across sources. This task introduces two main challenges: (i) combining multiple sources into a coherent narrative requires identifying cross-paper relationships and synthesizing their content, and (ii) the desired organization and emphasis varies substantially across presenters, making personalization essential for user satisfaction. Motivated by these challenges, we propose KeyDeck, the first multi-PTSG framework that integrates per-paper and cross-paper analysis to identify relationships among sources and synthesize their contributions into a coherent narrative, and reference-guided personalization to infer presenters' narrative, content, textual, and visual preferences and adapt slide generation accordingly. To evaluate generation quality, personalization adherence, and conversational goal satisfaction, we introduce KeyBench, a benchmark of 100 expert-authored multi-paper presentations, together with a comprehensive evaluation framework. Our results demonstrate that KeyDeck outperforms existing approaches in multi-PTSG quality, personalization adherence, and conversational goal satisfaction.

Embodied-VGGT: Preserving Reliable Local Geometry for Long-Sequence Reconstruction

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Long sequence reconstruction must correct accumulated drift while preserving locally consistent geometry. In robotics and autonomous driving, embodied acquisition provides recurring platform- environment geometry and repeated observations of scene structure and platform motion. We present Embodied-VGGT, which exploits these cues to establish reliable local relationships and selectively preserve them during global refinement. Memory-Augmented Grounded Chunks (MAGC) builds on Scal3R's global context memory and uses relative scale references derived from predicted ground geometry to regularize test-time memory updates, encouraging cross-chunk consistency while limiting changes to reliable local geometry. Superpoint Alignment (SA) and Cross-Loop Optimization (CL) jointly address the robust establishment and preservation of geometric relationships. SA balances ground and non-ground evidence across spatial regions to estimate inter-chunk translation. CL uses geometric and motion consistency across interleaved windows to select coherent chunk groups, each receiving a shared Sim(3) correction that preserves its internal relative transformations during global optimization. Experiments on five public driving benchmarks demonstrate consistent and substantial improvements in long sequence reconstruction. On KITTI Odometry, Embodied-VGGT achieves a mean absolute trajectory error of 7.30 m and a Chamfer distance of 2.67 m, reducing these errors by 46.8% and 23.3% compared with the state-of-the-art baseline under same evaluation protocol. To facilitate reproducibility, we released our code: https://github.com/Ang-Sheep/Embodied-VGGT.

What a Drop Reveals: Sim-to-Real Material Estimation for Deformable Objects from Video

📅 2026-11-25 18:47
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

How an object deforms when it hits the ground reveals what it is made of. From a single real video of a drop, we estimate a deformable object's density-normalized stiffness $E/ρ$, and its yield stress $σ_Y/ρ$ when the impact causes plastic deformation, with a feed-forward predictor trained only in simulation. Real videos carry no material labels, so such predictors are developed and validated on synthetic data alone. We show that synthetic validation ranks their geometric inputs in the wrong order. Material acts through 3D deformation, and tracklets lifted to 3D with estimated depth are indeed the most accurate input in simulation. However, on real drops they are the least accurate, and better depth does not repair them: the failure persists with simulator depth in training, LiDAR depth at test time, and exact depth on rendered replays of the real drops. Short 2D optical-flow tracklets do the opposite: they improve nothing in simulation, yet they transfer best. Material is therefore better read from what perception measures consistently in both domains than from what is most informative in simulation. Our predictor, PhysReader, reads the video and 2D tracklets and learns the lift to 3D in simulation by matching a teacher that sees exact 3D deformation. To measure transfer, we introduce PhysReader-Real: 16 everyday objects dropped five times each, with reference materials fit jointly to each object's drops and checked against tensile tests. On PhysReader-Real, PhysReader is closer to the reference materials than every other input and baseline. The one exception is a per-scene optimizer started from PhysReader's prediction, which ends slightly closer but takes 49 minutes per video instead of 12 seconds.

Counter-DECEIVR: Counterfactual Explanations for Deepfake Detectors using Detector-Guided Rectified Flow Inversion

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Deep-learning-generated media are increasingly passed off as real, driving an arms race between image generators and deepfake detectors. Trust in the latter is limited by their often black-box nature; and, while region-based explanations can sometimes be extracted, these become less useful as generation quality improves. Counterfactual examples, which show the minimal change that flips a detector's decision, reveal what evidence drives a verdict, helping analysts decide whether to trust it. This work introduces Counter-DECEIVR (DEtector-guided reCtifiEd flow InVeRsion for counterfactuals), a novel training-free framework that generates counterfactual examples. Counter-DECEIVR steers latents in the velocity field of a rectified flow model using gradients from off-the-shelf deepfake detectors, without training a generator or adapting the detector; unlike prior methods, it requires no per-instance or per-detector fitting and no diffusion training on the target data. It also leverages the detector's explanation maps to concentrate editing to salient regions, turning low-level feature importance into localised counterfactual edits. A single parameter controls the strength of the edits. Across two deepfake detectors and two widely used forensics datasets, Counter-DECEIVR outperforms prior counterfactual generation methods, including training-based ones. Code will be made available upon acceptance.

WorldInteract: Controllable Streaming Video Generation for Multi-Material Interactions

📅 2026-11-25 17:14
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Video world models aim to create interactive physical worlds that capture how diverse materials move and interact. Yet material-dependent dynamics remain underexplored in controllable video generation, limiting reliable control in scenes with different materials. We present WorldInteract, a streaming video generation framework that decomposes cross-material interaction into material-dependent behavior generation and contact response computation. External pushes and contact impulses are expressed through a shared velocity-increment interface, allowing material behaviors learned from interaction-free videos to be reused in interacting scenes. A physics controller predicts contacts and computes velocity increments from estimated masses and restitution coefficients, while a causal generator executes these increments using learned material behaviors. This separation enables cross-material interaction without supervised contact videos spanning diverse material pairings and contact configurations. We train the generator on 30k simulated interaction-free videos spanning 22 material classes and introduce Material-Aware RL, using verifiable physical rewards to refine material behavior and control execution. WorldInteract substantially outperforms state-of-the-art methods on Physics-IQ and delivers more reliable push control on real-world images.

Towards 360$^\circ$ Embodied UAV Tracking Without Turning

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Conventional embodied UAV tracking couples target observability with heading control because targets may leave the field of view of forward-facing cameras. We investigate a complementary paradigm that uses full-spherical perception to follow designated targets without active heading adjustment. To support this setting, we introduce 360UAV-ETrack, a simulation benchmark comprising 2,652 sequences, over 1.73 million synchronized observations, eight tracking tasks, and 150 held-out closed-loop scenarios. We propose 360UAV-TVLA, a panoramic vision--language--action (VLA) framework integrating persistent visual referring (PVR), compressed temporal memory (CTM), and geometry-aligned trajectory supervision (GTS) for instance identification, motion reasoning, and three-dimensional control. Experiments across two VLA backbones show consistent gains over standard fine-tuning, with normalized average tracking frames increasing from 22.56\% to 35.28\% for $π_0$ and from 25.68\% to 33.64\% for $π_{0.5}$. Our benchmark further reveals a substantial gap between offline imitation accuracy and closed-loop tracking reliability, establishing a challenging testbed for future research https://libingyu01.github.io/360UAV-ETrack_360UAV-TVLA/

Technical Report MEG2text: Beyond Alignment in Non-Invasive Brain-to-Text

📅 2026-11-25 16:47
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Non-invasive brain-to-text seeks to recover language from brain activity without surgery. A central challenge is turning learned brain-language representations into accurate word predictions. We present MEG2text, a decoding system that combines corrected training and evaluation, brain-text alignment, a class-balanced word readout, and target-word language-model fusion. On LibriBrain100's word-ranking task, MEG2text reaches 64.12% mean Top-10 balanced accuracy, improving on published MEG-XL by 16.92 percentage points across four corpora, with gains on each corpus. Controlled comparisons show that both implementation corrections and a learned readout contribute to this improvement. Candidate-level analysis reveals that some words receive informative scores yet consistently lose to competing candidates, while an identical-feature comparison shows that learning the classification rule improves decoding without changing the representation. These findings identify the decoding rule as a consequential part of non-invasive brain-to-text and show how system improvements can make better use of aligned neural features.

The AI Disclosure Paradox: Operational Disclosure and the Rise of Corporate AI Narratives

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

AI-related corporate disclosure can expand while becoming less concentrated on current use. Across U.S. SEC filings from 2017 to 2025, AI-related passage intensity increased 6.3-fold between 2017-2022 and 2023-2025, whereas corrected operational-disclosure intensity increased 3.3-fold. The operational share fell from 25.76% to 13.45%; approximately 89% of the net increase in passage intensity fell outside the operational category. The decline persists within sectors and filing types and in 10-K-only analysis. These estimates combine frozen language-model predictions for 26,279 AI-related passages with an audit of 1,000 annotated passages. The findings distinguish growth in organizational attention from growth in implementation-specific claims: more operational disclosure can coexist with a substantially lower operational share. Operational disclosure was thus diluted, not displaced, by nonoperational disclosure. Reading corporate technology narratives therefore requires attention to both disclosure intensity and the organizational state described.

HAS: Hierarchical Adaptive Frame Sampling for Video Understanding

📅 2026-11-25 15:23
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Due to context-length constraints, most MLLMs cannot process full-length videos and therefore rely on sampling a subset of frames as input. However, existing sampling methods, ranging from uniform sampling to relevance-based selection, are often driven by a single sampling principle and thus struggle to accommodate the heterogeneous evidential requirements of different queries: some demand a holistic understanding of how the video unfolds over time, while others hinge on fine-grained events within a short temporal window. To address this limitation, we propose \textbf{Hierarchical Adaptive Frame Sampling (HAS)}, a two-stage frame sampling framework. In the first stage, \emph{Backbone Frame Construction}, we apply a Determinantal Point Process (DPP) to sample frames that are both query-relevant and non-redundant. These selected frames capture key moments across the video and form the backbone of the sampling set, providing a foundation for subsequent enrichment. In the second stage, \emph{Adaptive Contextual Enrichment}, we analyze the temporal distribution of the backbone frames to infer the query's requirements and adaptively allocate the remaining frame budget between Local and Global Context. The Local Context enriches the backbone with fine-grained temporal dynamics and short-range causal relations, whereas the Global Context connects temporally isolated evidence and provides a holistic view of the entire video to support global understanding. Through such a hierarchical two-stage process, HAS effectively addresses diverse query requirements. Incorporated into three leading MLLMs, it demonstrates consistently superior performance across the Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU benchmarks.

HOG-WAM: Improving Visual Robustness through Future Gradient Prediction

📅 2026-11-25 17:59
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet visual corruption can disrupt the resulting policies. We introduce HOG-WAM, which adds future Histograms of Oriented Gradients (HOG) as structural supervision derived directly from demonstration images. An auxiliary HOG diffusion transformer (DiT) module reads RGB features through asymmetric attention, allowing its objective to shape the visual representation used for control. The module is used only during training and removed at inference, preserving FastWAM's RGB-to-action architecture. We evaluate four controlled observation corruptions through a visual-corruption extension of RoboTwin, termed RoboTwin-Plus; HOG-WAM achieves the highest success among the compared policies under all four. Mixed-noise success increases from 78.86% to 82.86% relative to FastWAM, with comparable performance on clean scenes. On LIBERO Plus, overall success increases from 74.49% to 75.26%, with the largest gain under sensor noise, from 74.14% to 78.89%. Across four Franka tasks, average success increases from 40% to 59% under clean observations and from 17% to 24% under noisy observations. A representation ablation favors combining RGB observations with auxiliary HOG prediction over replacing RGB with HOG. These findings support future-gradient prediction as complementary supervision for visually robust control, with larger absolute gains on physical tasks than in simulation. Code is available at https://github.com/iPPLab/HOG-WAM.

TIDE-Flow: Fine-Grained Temporal Evidence and Structured Displacement Matching for Spike Optical Flow

📅 2026-11-25 18:26
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Spike cameras capture high-speed motion through an integrate-and-fire mechanism, providing fine-grained temporal observations without exposure blur. However, their binary streams encode accumulated illumination rather than instantaneous intensity, making reliable motion estimation challenging. Existing spike-based methods exploit temporal aggregation to construct motion representations, but this process may weaken fine-grained firing patterns. Meanwhile, correlation-based estimators often rely on learned matching features without explicitly characterizing the spatial distribution and ambiguity of candidate displacements. To address these challenges, we propose TIDE-Flow, a spike-based optical flow estimation framework that jointly exploits Temporal Integration Evidence (TIE) and Displacement Evidence Encoding (DEE). TIE combines raw spike patterns, multi-scale firing statistics, temporal moments, and boundary-aware interval cues, preserving complementary temporal information before spatial feature compression. DEE further integrates structured candidate-grid features with explicit displacement statistics to characterize matching geometry and competing correspondences. Both components are incorporated into a difference-correlation recurrent refinement framework, enabling effective temporal motion modeling and correspondence estimation. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of TIDE-Flow, with controlled ablations validating the contributions of temporal and displacement evidence modeling.

GeoCAM: Self-Supervised Sonar-Optical Representation Learning via Geographic Overlap Supervision

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Cross-modal self-supervised learning for paired side-scan sonar and underwater optical imagery is a promising direction for benthic habitat understanding, but it remains underexplored because existing methods do not fully exploit the one-to-many correspondences and geographic metadata available in real opti-acoustic surveys. This limits the ability to learn representations that are both semantically meaningful and geometrically consistent from abundant unlabeled marine data. To address this problem, we present GeoCAM, a geography-guided framework that learns a shared embedding space for sonar tiles and their associated optical views without semantic annotations. GeoCAM achieves its goal by combining three components: (i) a Set Attention Aggregator that adaptively summarizes multiple optical observations into a single tile-level representation; (ii) a geometry-weighted InfoNCE objective that converts GPS-derived footprint overlap into soft positive supervision; and (iii) an optional cross-modal masked reconstruction branch that encourages physically grounded cross-modal alignment. Experiments on the BenthiCat SSS-CAM dataset show that GeoCAM can be effectively exploited to learn which sonar and camera samples describe the same seabed patch, improving cross-modal retrieval and alignment over strong contrastive baselines. Code is available at https://github.com/FadiZidiDz/GeoCAM.

Beyond Pose Control: Anatomically Consistent Ultrasound Video Generation

📅 2026-11-25 16:08
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Camera-controlled video generation anticipates how a scene appears from new viewpoints by relying on a prior learned implicitly from large-scale data. Ultrasound poses the same problem, with the probe pose as the viewpoint, but under opposite conditions: anatomy offers an explicit prior that every sonographer relies on, whereas tracked scans are too scarce to learn it implicitly. As a result, pose control alone is not enough, and pose-conditioned ultrasound generators produce implausible anatomy in unscanned regions and inconsistent anatomy on revisits. We go beyond pose control and give the generator the two kinds of knowledge that make up a sonographer's mental model. An anatomical prior, built from a population of subjects, guides generation where nothing has been scanned, and is refined online by every generated chunk towards the scanned subject. A 3D latent memory, designed around the cross-sectional physics of ultrasound, compounds the generated slices into a volume of the patient and reslices it at new poses, keeping revisited anatomy consistent. The fine-grained memory enters the generator at the input, while the coarse anatomy prior guides the layout across transformer blocks. Experiments on thyroid, kidney, and lumbar spine show improved visual fidelity, pose following, and anatomical consistency over pose-conditioned and memory-augmented baselines.

Shape-Aware Three-Way Merge for RDF Knowledge Graphs: The schema defines the conflict: an implementation and evaluation

📅 2026-11-26 14:33
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Merging RDF serializations creates ordering conflicts and misses semantic ones. We describe and evaluate a three-way merge in which the schema defines a conflict: set algebra combines canonical triples, and a slot is contended only where a SHACL shape bounds its cardinality and both sides' changes cannot stand within that bound. Unbounded predicates union; contended slots go to a person. The same shapes graph validates the result. We do not claim the first three-way merge for RDF. Our contribution is an implementation in a production multi-agent store and an evaluation against a reimplementation of Quit Store's Context Merge overlap rule, which it extends rather than replaces, and six other strategies. Across five synthetic seeds, the shape-aware operator raised 33 conflicts, all true positives as judged by the same shapes that define them, with no triples lost or fabricated and no SHACL violations admitted. The context-overlap baseline, counted per slot, detected the same 33 conflicts but asked 845 questions. A replay built from recorded production incidents covers 105 human alias repairs (93 after excluding 12 chained pairs) and 939 duplicate-value incidents. It reproduces a triple-level limit: different names for one entity remain invisible (0 of 93 alias repairs detected, matching 0 of 21 synthetic cases). In a study of evolving shape contracts, merging shape declarations first handles the tested cardinality changes, while datatype evolution exposes a detector blind spot caught by post-merge validation. An oracle that reads no shapes agrees with the shipped operator on the facts and history of all 23 reachable single-slot cases. Under wrong shapes, a missing bound loses data silently and a spurious one only adds questions. The supported claim is fewer human decisions with nothing silently lost, for cardinality conflicts in one deployment, not general merge correctness.

Angular Interpolation in Sparse-View Low-Dose CT Reconstruction: Placement Effects and Data-Consistency Step-Size Scaling

📅 2026-11-25 14:57
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

Dual-domain networks combine projection and image processing for sparse-view low-dose computed tomography (CT). We quantify the contribution of angular interpolation before learning and distinguish its roles in sinogram initialisation and the image residual base. On synthetic parallel-beam data, parameter-free interpolation reduces noise-free missing-view mean absolute error by 99.7% relative to zero filling at 60 views. Full-view filtered back-projection (FBP) of the jointly learned completed sinogram adds 1.27 dB of peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) over interpolated FBP. In a data-consistency dual-domain network (DC-DuNet), a 2x2 configuration ablation controls branch width, depth and training schedule while varying these placements and their associated input channels. After 20 epochs, the PSNR contrasts are +1.98 dB for sinogram initialisation and -2.16 dB for the interpolated image base; the image-base ranking also reverses during training. The sinogram-initialised variant exceeds an image-only U-Net by 0.96 dB at 60 views and 10% dose, and by 1.28 dB on independently generated synthetic data. The U-Net leads at higher view counts and with a shared interpolated image base. Without retraining, the variant retains a 0.12 dB advantage on held-out CHAOS abdominal CT under simulated degradation; the image-base contrast rounds to zero. The implemented projection/backprojection pair has empirical dominant gain of approximately 0.85NK, where N is image-grid width and K acquired view count. These results distinguish interpolation placement from network architecture and support NK normalisation for step calibration.

BOP-Refer: Benchmark for Monocular 3D and 2D Object Grounding

📅 2026-11-25 18:29
arXivHTMLPDFKimi

We introduce BOP-Refer, the first benchmark for monocular 3D and 2D grounding of everyday and industrial objects sourced from 9 diverse BOP datasets. We pair images from these datasets with VLM-generated and human-verified referring expressions, and derive accurate ground-truth 3D and 2D bounding boxes of the referred objects from their 3D models and 6DoF pose annotations. Our systematic evaluation of 17 frontier VLMs across the major proprietary and open-weight families (Gemini, GPT, Claude, Qwen, Gemma) shows that the best model, Gemini 4 Carbon, reaches 72.6% AP_IoU2D in 2D but only 12.4% AP_IoU3D in 3D, despite the more lenient IoU thresholds in 3D. The predicted 3D boxes are usually centered on the target objects in the image but often off in depth by more than the object's size, suggesting that metric scale estimation, which needs reliable object size priors, is currently by far the main source of error.
